The blocked scheme\n    // below performs exactly the same sequence of comparisons/updates on every\n    // element, so both dist and path are bit-identical to the sequential version:\n    //   For a block of KB pivots [kb, ke):\n    //   1. Update the pivot rows [kb, ke) for k in [kb, ke) in order (parallel\n    //      over columns), saving a snapshot of each pivot row k right before\n    //      iteration k is applied to it.\n    //   2. Update all other rows (parallel over rows) for k in [kb, ke) in order,\n    //      reading pivot row values from the snapshots.\n    const long n = static_cast<long>(numNodes);\n    if (n == 0) return;\n\n    unsigned int* __restrict d = dist.data();\n    unsigned int* __restrict p = path.data();\n\n    // Snapshots of pivot rows (KBLOCK x n) and pivot-column values of the pivot\n    // rows (KBLOCK x KBLOCK), both at stage k-1.\n    std::vector<unsigned int> snap(static_cast<size_t>(KBLOCK) * n);\n    unsigned int* __restrict S = snap.data();\n    unsigned int A[KBLOCK][KBLOCK];\n\n    #pragma omp parallel default(none) shared(d, p, n, S, A) proc_bind(close)\n    {\n        for (long kb = 0; kb < n; kb += KBLOCK) {\n            const long ke = std::min(kb + KBLOCK, n);\n            const long kn = ke - kb;\n\n            // Step 0: diagonal block (pivot rows x pivot columns), sequential.\n            #pragma omp single\n            {\n                for (long k = kb; k < ke; ++k) {\n                    const unsigned int* rowK = d + k * n;\n                    for (long j = kb; j < ke; ++j) S[(k - kb) * n + j] = rowK[j];\n                    for (long i = kb; i < ke; ++i) {\n                        unsigned int* rowI = d + i * n;\n                        const unsigned int distIK = rowI[k];\n                        A[i - kb][k - kb] = distIK;\n                        relaxSegment(rowI, p + i * n, S + (k - kb) * n, distIK,\n                                     static_cast<unsigned int>(k), kb, ke);\n                    }\n                }\n            } // implicit barrier\n\n            // Step 1: pivot rows, remaining columns; parallel over column tiles.\n            {\n                const long rest = n - kn;\n                constexpr long CT = 256;\n                const long ntiles = (rest + CT - 1) / CT;\n                #pragma omp for schedule(static)\n                for (long t = 0; t < ntiles; ++t) {\n                    long c0 = t * CT;\n                    long c1 = std::min(c0 + CT, rest);\n                    // Map [0, rest) onto columns outside [kb, ke)\n                    for (long seg = 0; seg < 2; ++seg) {\n                        long j0, j1;\n                        if (seg == 0) {\n                            j0 = c0; j1 = std::min(c1, kb);\n                        } else {\n                            j0 = std::max(c0, kb) + kn; j1 = c1 + kn;\n                        }\n                        if (j0 >= j1) continue;\n                        for (long k = kb; k < ke; ++k) {\n                            const unsigned int* rowK = d + k * n;\n                            unsigned int* sK = S + (k - kb) * n;\n                            for (long j = j0; j < j1; ++j) sK[j] = rowK[j];\n                            for (long i = kb; i < ke; ++i) {\n                                relaxSegment(d + i * n, p + i * n, sK, A[i - kb][k - kb],\n                                             static_cast<unsigned int>(k), j0, j1);\n                            }\n                        }\n                    }\n                }\n            } // implicit barrier\n\n            // Step 2: all non-pivot rows; parallel over rows.\n            #pragma omp for schedule(static)\n            for (long i = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n                if (i >= kb && i < ke) continue;\n                unsigned int* __restrict rowI = d + i * n;\n                unsigned int* __restrict pathI = p + i * n;\n\n                // Pivot columns first, recording dist[i][k] at stage k-1.\n                unsigned int aik[KBLOCK];\n                for (long k = kb; k < ke; ++k) {\n                    aik[k - kb] = rowI[k];\n                    relaxSegment(rowI, pathI, S + (k - kb) * n, rowI[k],\n                                 static_cast<unsigned int>(k), kb, ke);\n                }\n\n                // Remaining columns, tiled for cache reuse across the k block.\n                for (long t0 = 0; t0 < n; t0 += JTILE) {\n                    const long t1 = std::min(t0 + JTILE, n);\n                    for (long seg = 0; seg < 2; ++seg) {\n                        const long j0 = seg == 0 ? t0 : std::max(t0, ke);\n                        const long j1 = seg == 0 ? std::min(t1, kb) : t1;\n                        if (j0 >= j1) continue;\n                        for (long k = kb; k < ke; ++k) {\n                            relaxSegment(rowI, pathI, S + (k - kb) * n, aik[k - kb],\n                                         static_cast<unsigned int>(k), j0, j1);\n                        }\n                    }\n                }\n            } // implicit barrier\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n'''\ns=s.replace(old,new)\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nEOF\ngrep -n \"single\\|A\\[\" floydwarshall.cpp | head"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"container":null,"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":903,"cache_read_input_tokens":27612,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":903},"output_tokens":4,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"89e6d66a-5356-4fdb-9ee0-0b16b9ad7853","uuid":"a3aa3eb6-6f51-401d-bc75-b684508f98d4","timestamp":"2026-10-04T08:05:36.240Z","request_id":"req_011CfgrG19aThag9JtQeCT7D"}

newDist : rowI[j];\n        pathI[j] = better ? k : pathI[j];\n    }\n}\n\n// Apply iterations kb..kb+kn-1 (in order) to an RB x VW block at column j.\n// aik[r * KBLOCK + kk] holds dist[i_r][kb+kk] as it was before iteration kb+kk.\nstatic inline void relaxRegisterBlock(unsigned int* const* rows, unsigned int* const* paths,\n                                      const unsigned int* __restrict S, const long n,\n                                      const unsigned int* __restrict aik,\n                                      const long kb, const long kn, const long j) {\n    vu8 dv[RB], pv[RB];\n    for (long r = 0; r < RB; ++r) {\n        dv[r] = loadu(rows[r] + j);\n        pv[r] = loadu(paths[r] + j);\n    }\n    for (long kk = 0; kk < kn; ++kk) {\n        const vu8 sk = loadu(S + kk * n + j);\n        const vu8 kv = vu8{} + static_cast<unsigned int>(kb + kk);\n        for (long r = 0; r < RB; ++r) {\n            const vu8 nd = aik[r * KBLOCK + kk] + sk;\n            const vu8 mn = (nd < dv[r]) ? nd : dv[r];   // dist update (strictly-less)\n            pv[r] = (mn == dv[r]) ? pv[r] : kv;         // path set to k iff improved\n            dv[r] = mn;\n        }\n    }\n    for (long r = 0; r < RB; ++r) {\n        storeu(rows[r] + j, dv[r]);\n        storeu(paths[r] + j, pv[r]);\n    }\n}\n\nvoid floydWarshall(std::vector<unsigned int>& dist, \n                   std::vector<unsigned int>& path, \n                   const size_t numNodes) {\n    // Floyd-Warshall, parallelized with OpenMP and blocked over k.\n    //\n    // The classic algorithm updates every element (i,j) for k = 0..n-1 in order,\n    // using dist[i][k] and dist[k][j] as they are after iteration k-1 (which equal\n    // their values after iteration k, since dist[k][k] == 0). The blocked scheme\n    // below performs exactly the same sequence of comparisons/updates on every\n    // element, so both dist and path are bit-identical to the sequential version:\n    //   For a block of pivots [kb, ke):\n    //   0. Diagonal block (pivot rows x pivot columns), in order, recording the\n    //      pivot-column values A[i][k] and pivot-row snapshots at stage k-1.\n    //   1. Pivot rows, remaining columns (parallel over column tiles), saving a\n    //      snapshot of each pivot row k right before iteration k is applied.\n    //   2. All other rows (parallel over rows) for k in [kb, ke) in order,\n    //      reading pivot row values from the snapshots.\n    const long n = static_cast<long>(numNodes);\n    if (n == 0) return;\n\n    // NUMA-friendly working copies: first-touched by the threads that own the rows.\n    const size_t nn = numNodes * numNodes;\n    std::unique_ptr<unsigned int[]> dBuf(new unsigned int[nn]);\n    std::unique_ptr<unsigned int[]> pBuf(new unsigned int[nn]);\n    unsigned int* __restrict d = dBuf.get();\n    unsigned int* __restrict p = pBuf.get();\n    unsigned int* const dSrc = dist.data();\n    unsigned int* const pSrc = path.data();\n\n    // Snapshots of pivot rows (KBLOCK x n) and pivot-column values of the pivot\n    // rows (KBLOCK x KBLOCK), both at stage k-1.\n    std::unique_ptr<unsigned int[]> snap(new unsigned int[static_cast<size_t>(KBLOCK) * n]);\n    unsigned int* __restrict S = snap.get();\n    unsigned int A[KBLOCK][KBLOCK];\n\n    const long numGroups = (n + RB - 1) / RB;\n\n    #pragma omp parallel default(none) shared(d, p, dSrc, pSrc, n, S, A, numGroups) proc_bind(close)\n    {\n        // Contiguous partition of row groups among threads\n        const long nth = omp_get_num_threads();\n        const long tid = omp_get_thread_num();\n        const long g0 = numGroups * tid / nth;\n        const long g1 = numGroups * (tid + 1) / nth;\n        const long r0 = std::min(g0 * RB, n);\n        const long r1 = std::min(g1 * RB, n);\n\n        for (long i = r0; i < r1; ++i) {\n            std::memcpy(d + i * n, dSrc + i * n, n * sizeof(unsigned int));\n            std::memcpy(p + i * n, pSrc + i * n, n * sizeof(unsigned int));\n        }\n        // aik values for this thread's rows (stage k-1 pivot-column values)\n        std::vector<unsigned int> aikBuf(static_cast<size_t>(r1 - r0) * KBLOCK);\n        unsigned int* const aik = aikBuf.data();\n        #pragma omp barrier\n\n        for (long kb = 0; kb < n; kb += KBLOCK) {\n            const long ke = std::min(kb + KBLOCK, n);\n            const long kn = ke - kb;\n\n            // Step 0: diagonal block, sequential.\n            #pragma omp single\n            {\n                for (long k = kb; k < ke; ++k) {\n                    const unsigned int* rowK = d + k * n;\n                    for (long j = kb; j < ke; ++j) S[(k - kb) * n + j] = rowK[j];\n                    for (long i = kb; i < ke; ++i) {\n                        unsigned int* rowI = d + i * n;\n                        const unsigned int distIK = rowI[k];\n                        A[i - kb][k - kb] = distIK;\n                        relaxSegment(rowI, p + i * n, S + (k - kb) * n, distIK,\n                                     static_cast<unsigned int>(k), kb, ke);\n                    }\n                }\n            } // implicit barrier\n\n            // Step 1: pivot rows, remaining columns; parallel over column tiles.\n            {\n                const long rest = n - kn;\n                const long ntiles = (rest + CTILE - 1) / CTILE;\n                #pragma omp for schedule(static)\n                for (long t = 0; t < ntiles; ++t) {\n                    const long c0 = t * CTILE;\n                    const long c1 = std::min(c0 + CTILE, rest);\n                    // Map [0, rest) onto columns outside [kb, ke)\n                    for (int seg = 0; seg < 2; ++seg) {\n                        const long j0 = seg == 0 ? c0 : std::max(c0, kb) + kn;\n                        const long j1 = seg == 0 ? std::min(c1, kb) : c1 + kn;\n                        if (j0 >= j1) continue;\n                        for (long k = kb; k < ke; ++k) {\n                            const unsigned int* rowK = d + k * n;\n                            unsigned int* sK = S + (k - kb) * n;\n                            for (long j = j0; j < j1; The blocked scheme\n    // below performs exactly the same sequence of comparisons/updates on every\n    // element, so both dist and path are bit-identical to the sequential version:\n    //   For a block of pivots [kb, ke):\n    //   0. Diagonal block (pivot rows x pivot columns), in order, recording the\n    //      pivot-column values A[i][k] and pivot-row snapshots at stage k-1.\n    //   1. Pivot rows, remaining columns (parallel over column tiles), saving a\n    //      snapshot of each pivot row k right before iteration k is applied.\n    //   2. All other rows (parallel over rows) for k in [kb, ke) in order,\n    //      reading pivot row values from the snapshots.\n    const long n = static_cast<long>(numNodes);\n    if (n == 0) return;\n\n    // NUMA-friendly working copies: first-touched by the threads that own the rows.\n    const size_t nn = numNodes * numNodes;\n    std::unique_ptr<unsigned int[]> dBuf(new unsigned int[nn]);\n    std::unique_ptr<unsigned int[]> pBuf(new unsigned int[nn]);\n    unsigned int* __restrict d = dBuf.get();\n    unsigned int* __restrict p = pBuf.get();\n    unsigned int* const dSrc = dist.data();\n    unsigned int* const pSrc = path.data();\n\n    // Snapshots of pivot rows (KBLOCK x n) and pivot-column values of the pivot\n    // rows (KBLOCK x KBLOCK), both at stage k-1.\n    std::unique_ptr<unsigned int[]> snap(new unsigned int[static_cast<size_t>(KBLOCK) * n]);\n    unsigned int* __restrict S = snap.get();\n    unsigned int A[KBLOCK][KBLOCK];\n\n    const long numGroups = (n + RB - 1) / RB;\n\n    #pragma omp parallel default(none) shared(d, p, dSrc, pSrc, n, S, A, numGroups) proc_bind(close)\n    {\n        // Contiguous partition of row groups among threads\n        const long nth = omp_get_num_threads();\n        const long tid = omp_get_thread_num();\n        const long g0 = numGroups * tid / nth;\n        const long g1 = numGroups * (tid + 1) / nth;\n        const long r0 = std::min(g0 * RB, n);\n        const long r1 = std::min(g1 * RB, n);\n\n        for (long i = r0; i < r1; ++i) {\n            std::memcpy(d + i * n, dSrc + i * n, n * sizeof(unsigned int));\n            std::memcpy(p + i * n, pSrc + i * n, n * sizeof(unsigned int));\n        }\n        // aik values for this thread's rows (stage k-1 pivot-column values)\n        std::vector<unsigned int> aikBuf(static_cast<size_t>(r1 - r0) * KBLOCK);\n        unsigned int* const aik = aikBuf.data();\n        #pragma omp barrier\n\n        for (long kb = 0; seg < 2; ++seg) {\n                        const long j0 = seg == 0 ? c0 : std::max(c0, kb) + kn;\n                        const long j1 = seg == 0 ? std::min(c1, kb) : c1 + kn;\n                        if (j0 >= j1) continue;\n                        for (long k = kb; k < ke; ++k) {\n                            const unsigned int* rowK = d + k * n;\n                            unsigned int* sK = S + (k - kb) * n;\n                            for (long j = j0; j < j1; i < r1; ++i) {\n                if (i >= kb && i < ke) continue;\n                unsigned int* rowI = d + i * n;\n                unsigned int* pathI = p + i * n;\n                unsigned int* a = aik + (i - r0) * KBLOCK;\n                for (long k = kb; {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5-5","id":"msg_011CfgsztkRQ6tkc73G2PMCX","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The Floyd–Warshall benchmark now runs in parallel with OpenMP, and its output is identical to the original. Only `floydwarshall/floydwarshall.cpp` and `floydwarshall/CMakeLists.txt` changed, and the executable is still `floydwarshall`.\n\n**Correctness:** I compared both the distance matrix and the path matrix against the original serial program for 1 to 3000 nodes, with several thread counts. They matched exactly every time (the path matrix was checked with a temporary hash, since the program only prints the distances). `-v` validation passes, and a clean CMake build gives no warnings.\n\n**Speed** (2-socket, 128-core EPYC 7763, 256 threads):\n\n| nodes | original (1 thread) | now (all threads) |\n|---|---|---|\n| 512 | 165 ms | about 6–17 ms |\n| 2048 | 9.4 s | about 40–55 ms |\n| 4096 | — | about 150–165 ms |\n| 8192 | — | about 0.85 s |\n\nThe machine was shared with other jobs (load average 10–80), so these numbers are best-of-3 and vary from run to run. On one thread the new code is already about 8× faster than the original, because of the vectorized inner loop. At 4096 nodes it scales from 8.8 s on 1 thread to 147 ms on 128 threads (60×).\n\n**What changed:**\n- **Blocked processing that keeps the original update order.** The matrix is processed 32 intermediate nodes at a time instead of one. Each cell still gets the same comparisons in the same order with the same values as the original, which is why the path matrix also matches exactly. A standard blocked Floyd–Warshall gives the same distances but can pick different paths when two paths tie.\n- **Parallel work split.** A small 32×32 corner is done by one thread. The 32 pivot rows are split by column across threads. All other rows are split among threads in groups of 4. This takes three thread syncs per block of 32.\n- **Fast inner loop.** A 4-row × 8-column block of distances and paths stays in AVX2 registers for all 32 steps, with branch-free updates.\n- **Memory layout.** The work happens in padded, 64-byte-aligned copies, created by the threads that use them so data sits on the right memory node. Padding avoids slow cache behaviour at power-of-two sizes; at 8192 nodes it took the time from 1.97 s to 1.11 s.\n- **Thread pinning.** If neither `OMP_PLACES` nor `GOMP_CPU_AFFINITY` is set, each thread pins itself to one CPU (Linux only, through glibc). Without this, `proc_bind` does nothing in GCC's OpenMP runtime, and runs were 1.3–3× slower. Setting either variable hands placement back to the runtime.\n- **Path initialization.** `initializePathMatrix` is now a parallel loop that writes the original's final values directly.\n- **Build.** `CMakeLists.txt` now requires OpenMP (`find_package(OpenMP REQUIRED)`) and links `OpenMP::OpenMP_CXX`.\n\n**Limits:**\n- Updating the pivot rows still takes about 15% of the time at 8192 nodes, because those rows have to move between cores' caches each block.\n- The vector-type syntax in the inner loop is GCC/Clang-specific, so other compilers won't build it as-is."}],"container":null,"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":864,"cache_read_input_tokens":109707,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":864},"output_tokens":9,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"89e6d66a-5356-4fdb-9ee0-0b16b9ad7853","uuid":"ec9fb4ca-fcb3-4290-8ed8-f68235d077f9","timestamp":"2026-10-04T08:27:34.126Z","request_id":"req_011Cfgszrw3CmqBPeSUUCQbQ"}

They matched exactly every time (the path matrix was checked with a temporary hash, since the program only prints the distances). `-v` validation passes, and a clean CMake build gives no warnings.\n\n**Speed** (2-socket, 128-core EPYC 7763, 256 threads):\n\n| nodes | original (1 thread) | now (all threads) |\n|---|---|---|\n| 512 | 165 ms | about 6–17 ms |\n| 2048 | 9.4 s | about 40–55 ms |\n| 4096 | — | about 150–165 ms |\n| 8192 | — | about 0.85 s |\n\nThe machine was shared with other jobs (load average 10–80), so these numbers are best-of-3 and vary from run to run. On one thread the new code is already about 8× faster than the original, because of the vectorized inner loop. At 4096 nodes it scales from 8.8 s on 1 thread to 147 ms on 128 threads (60×).\n\n**What changed:**\n- **Blocked processing that keeps the original update order.** The matrix is processed 32 intermediate nodes at a time instead of one. Each cell still gets the same comparisons in the same order with the same values as the original, which is why the path matrix also matches exactly. A standard blocked Floyd–Warshall gives the same distances but can pick different paths when two paths tie.\n- **Parallel work split.** A small 32×32 corner is done by one thread. The 32 pivot rows are split by column across threads. All other rows are split among threads in groups of 4. This takes three thread syncs per block of 32.\n- **Fast inner loop.** A 4-row × 8-column block of distances and paths stays in AVX2 registers for all 32 steps, with branch-free updates.\n- **Memory layout.** The work happens in padded, 64-byte-aligned copies, created by the threads that use them so data sits on the right memory node. Padding avoids slow cache behaviour at power-of-two sizes; at 8192 nodes it took the time from 1.97 s to 1.11 s.\n- **Thread pinning.** If neither `OMP_PLACES` nor `GOMP_CPU_AFFINITY` is set, each thread pins itself to one CPU (Linux only, through glibc). Without this, `proc_bind` does nothing in GCC's OpenMP runtime, and runs were 1.3–3× slower. Setting either variable hands placement back to the runtime.\n- **Path initialization.** `initializePathMatrix` is now a parallel loop that writes the original's final values directly.\n- **Build.** `CMakeLists.txt` now requires OpenMP (`find_package(OpenMP REQUIRED)`) and links `OpenMP::OpenMP_CXX`.\n\n**Limits:**\n- Updating the pivot rows still takes about 15% of the time at 8192 nodes, because those rows have to move between cores' caches each block.\n- The vector-type syntax in the inner loop is GCC/Clang-specific, so other compilers won't build it as-is.","ttft_ms":2147,"type":"result","duration_ms":1465387,"uuid":"92bc318b-b8c4-4048-8f03-d3ff8d3f418f","ttft_stream_ms":997,"time_to_request_ms":137,"first_content_frame_ms":997,"queued_turn_count":0,"result_index":0}
